ParlaMint-AT_2005-04-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00104_d7e7252	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Mr State Secretary! High house! If something has been successful in objective consideration of this government, it will remain the first point that this government has made a trend change in family policy and is also far more aware of the demographic foundations of the state than the previous governments were. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] In any case, this government has made a paradigm change and has faced the seriousness that deserves this problem, which the previous speakers did not necessarily give in to the social debate and to the family debate. This is also the point to be confirmed in each of these debates. So far – up to the entry of the liberal into the government in particular – governments in Austria have always had a blind spot where a politician must have a demographic awareness. There is no economic and social policy, without the demographic conditions – that is, the people who will live now and in ten, 20, 30 and 40 years – being put into circulation! Furthermore, there is no economic and social policy without ensuring that population development is one that can be achieved! All the problems that the social state now presents to us – if we are to maintain it – simply have a root: not knowing, not knowing that demographic development is one that will force us – demographic developments that have gone wrong for a quarter of a century cannot be corrected for three quarters of a century – to achieve massive losses in many interests. This government has faced this challenge and has been aware of it. There have been a number of measures that should strengthen families, which has been successful, and measures that should enable families to fulfil a child’s desire – if they had one – which has also been successful. Family policy is important for two reasons: firstly, as a demographic necessity for each state. No state can behave neutrally in this respect. No family policy is also a policy and has a negative impact on the demographic basis. The second moment, of course, is that of justice. Justice on this issue is not so much within the family – it is necessary, but also a strong individual thing – but among the families, who, for the benefit of the general public – for their own pleasure, of course, too – the task and the burden of raising children in the social state, and the people who, for whatever reason, do not want to accept or cannot accept these burdens, at least do not have these burdens. – By the way, this is also the core statement of the family burden compensation law. This justice is what it is all about. Just a short example to illustrate this: Two young couples travel together on vacation, are in the same income class and spend their leisure time together. Then one couple happens to be a little gratifying, but which, in a material way, represents a disaster: The couple gets a child, a second, possibly a third. The common holidays must be maintained. For this, of course, there is no money in the family of those who then confess to children. The evenings together in stylish downtown restaurants are deleted. One of them is building a beautiful house, and the other may be able to make it with the help of grandparents. However, although they may have children and would have a single house with a garden, they may have to stay in a rental home and apply for rental aid. Then how is it going in old age? – Those who have renounced children have two pensions, spend the winter in Tenerife and still enjoy some world trips. The others will be satisfied with a pension and a little bit because at least one of the two parents had to give up the profession temporarily. Who is financing the luxury of those who enjoy it? – They are the children of the others! That's exactly the point. That's the fault of the social system that in our system of children only those who don't have them benefit! It is the task of every government to bring this about. More than all the other governments before, this government has done so. However, I would like to say: There's still a lot ahead of us and there's still a lot to do! But the most important thing is to recognize that too! There are still big gaps. Mr Oellinger, I have done my utmost to say this in plenary session: In the Committee on Budgets on Social Affairs, you have told me that it is precisely the government's confidence and, by the way, the unsocial awareness. – I said: Without children no social security! – You have that – so I think, because there can be no criticism of the sentence; that is “no na”! – designed as if I thought that each one should be cared for by his own children in the old age, and that would be exactly the non-social trend of the government. – This is an absolute misunderstanding! Without children no social security! [[Abg. Oils: No future!]] In archaic societies, this applies within the family between the physical children and the physical parents. In the social state, which raises this very principle – without children’s social security – to a state level and concludes the generation agreement between all boys and all the elderly, this applies all the more! If a single family – for whatever reason – no longer enjoys the observance of the generation contract, then it only meets it. But an entire state will find it when the social state is on this basis. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Matznetter.]] I exceeded my speaking time, but this issue concerns me because it is the core issue. Everything else will seem pointless in relation to this at some point. The following are to be concluded: I help myself with a quote of this – some journalists are really great! – what the Feuilleton chief of the “Frankfurter Allgemeine Zeitung” wrote these days: “We’re experiencing a... thriller and the writers are silent. Where do the criminal authorities stay?’ What do they say or why do they say that “at least two generations ” have been“ ruined ” in our rich West? And on top of that: "May the seas and animals once conspire against us,... or the ice once dominates the earth, as in the latest Hollywood productions. More closely, it would be to talk about what has and will happen to us, the citizens of the once most important economy of the earth. For a thriller is built up in such a way that a fever takes its course if never-man knows anything, and that the first ideas are scattered from those who need to know it better, until then for all things is too late." The same Frank Schirrmacher took over the patronship for a ten-part series in the “Frankfurter Allgemeine” on the topic “Demography” entitled “Thirty Years After Twelve”. “Thirty years after twelve” – that’s how it is, yet we have to face up to this task and cope with the development ahead of us. This does not save anyone, and if we are only somehow well advised, we must now, 30 years after twelve, try to continue to push forward a trend change.

[[Abg. Brosz: That's the right word!]] This is a compliment to Austrian transport policy with a focus on transport safety. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] The main areas of activity, in line with the European Union and its action programmes, are as follows: Compliance with speeds and distances, compliance with alcohol limits and the prohibition of drug use, use of restraint facilities, belts, child's seat. On the topic of belt and child seat a comparison: Austria is absolutely among the belts, because the screw rate in Austria is about 75 percent. On the European average, it is more than 90 percent, in good countries 90 percent, especially the Scandinavian countries. It should also be said that the risk of being seriously injured or killed in the event of a traffic accident is seven times higher if I am not hooked up than if I am secured by a restraint device. You can't say that often enough. We are also trying through targeted actions that already teach the children to realize that this is not something annoying, is a requirement that is disabled, but that it serves its own safety, in particular the safety of other road users. In the next few months, we will certainly set further priorities, particularly as regards restraint facilities and the use of belts and child seats, but also, as has been said, in terms of speed measurements, distance measurements, compliance with alcohol limits and the prohibition of drug use. The overall overview of the transport safety priorities is as follows: One of the road users' priorities is the introduction of the pre-eminence system. We've been discussing this for a long time. I also regard this as positive, because discussing systems and innovations to increase road safety is already a good thing in itself. And at this point I would also like to express my thanks and praise to the Austrian media world, because it is very much and very objectively reported, whenever we talk about road safety across parties and take appropriate measures. This reporting is very valuable, because it will be discussed and discussed. The multi-phase driving licence is also such a positive measure, and transport and mobility education, now also new for moped driving from 15, is also such a focus. Another area is traffic control and traffic monitoring. I would particularly like to mention the establishment of the transport influence system here. This is an installation which will be officially in operation at a first step in Tyrol in a few weeks, it is already in trial operation, a plant that will cost about €250 million and can affect traffic from Lake Constance to Lake Neusied, to particular circumstances such as wind, rain, rain, snow, even ghosts, traffic jams, so increased traffic frequency, accidents and the like. This traffic impact system not only serves to better manage traffic density, up to 10 percent without changing infrastructure, but also serves to reduce traffic accidents by up to 30 percent, according to estimates. This is already a reason for these traffic influences to be realized as soon as possible. Final expansion will be 2009, at the latest 2010. I mentioned the drug tests and alcohol precursors in the debate on the transport budget yesterday. They are also a preventive measure by the operator knowing that these testing devices enable even more efficient investigations or controls. In this area in particular, we have seen a sharp increase in the frequency of accidents and the resulting injuries, and this focus is also intended to significantly reduce the figures over the next few years. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Another focus is the improvement of traffic monitoring, in particular through road infrastructure and automatic monitoring facilities. The Section Control has already been mentioned here by Mr Wittauer, who is very well established. We will continue this and will announce it accordingly, because I believe that such measures should not serve the abcasher, as it is so well called, but should actually increase road safety. This will also be my intention in the future, just as I do not wish to punish, not to impose bans and commandments without numbers, but rather to promote awareness-building. This should start in childhood; it should be noted about the education of parents, the school, the middle school, but also in offers for seniors, how to make road transport safer. I would also like to mention the numerous improvements to the infrastructure that serves road safety. We have, for example, exchanged the control rail system, as Mr Wittauer has also mentioned: 1 700 kilometers of aluminium rails were installed, 240 million € were invested for this, which serves enormous safety. Of course, as far as road safety is concerned, the development of one-way counter-traffic tunnels in the high-level road network is of course a matter of course. We have recently invested a lot and will continue to implement appropriate expansion programmes in the next few years. Katschberg and Tauerntunnel are mentioned here, but also the recently commissioned Ambergtunnel in Vorarlberg, which now also has a second tube. At this point I can also deposit my intention to discuss a certain matter again soon, namely that we need to shorten the time from the decision of a political decision to realize something – in the infrastructure sector, for example, a second tunnel tube, which also serves safety – until construction is completed. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] We must find a good balance between the interests of the population concerned in the infrastructure, but also the interests of the road users and the overall interests. I think it is important that we come between decision-making and completion through procedural reductions and administrative simplification. Let me conclude by mentioning very briefly the accident documentation and investigation which we have also redesigned, with which we not only want to learn from our experience, but also to implement these findings, in future measures to avoid accidents. However, I would also like to mention the 26. KfG-Novelle with which “light a day” is to be introduced. The relevant review starts today. We have today sent the relevant proposals for these novels into consideration. It is intended to require general driving with light inside and out of town all day and all year round. Here too, there are international investigations, which suggest that the accident frequency can be reduced by just under 10 percent. The driver's licence reform was also mentioned, which improves driving licence training. It is about more efficient training, it is a new system of training courses, where the instruction is made possible with companions in particular. In turn, we are increasingly focusing on the awareness-building element in transport education and are thus on the right path. Ladies and gentlemen, I am convinced: If we follow these smaller numbers over the next few years, we will see that we are also on the right path to achieve the set target, namely reducing the number of road fatalities to half by 2010, reducing the number of road accidents by 20 percent. Thank you for your cooperation. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] If I then leave the debate early, it is not an expression of disinterest – I am very happy to discuss traffic safety with everyone – but has the reason that I am one of the representatives of the Republic of Austria at the funeral of the Holy Father in Rome. But I am convinced that my State Secretary, Mainoni, will represent me “trafficly” and worthy of this debate. Thank you.

I should like to point out one point: EuroRAP, an initiative of the European Union “Road Assessment Programmes”. It is possible for the first time to evaluate the overarching road network uniformly. I can also say – contrary to some reports – that Austria's overarching road network is getting very good notes there: Over 70 percent of the motorway network is rated with very good or good. We will, of course, pay particular attention to the accident priorities that can be found in the rural road network, on the national and municipal roads, namely that this EuroRAP measure, i.e. this examination of roads, of road pieces – one is approaching the subject not from the side of the vehicle driver, but from the nature of the road, the environment and so on – will also take place in the rural road network. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! We, the Austrian Federal Government, our ministry and the State Secretariat, are doing everything possible to reduce the number of accidents, because security is a priority.
